<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the fundamental group of every special compact Kähler manifold is virtually abelian, resolving Campana's abelianity conjecture in all dimensions. In particular, every smooth compact connected Kähler manifold of Kodaira dimension zero has virtually abelian ordinary fundamental group.

- **claim-abelianity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-abelianity)): Let $X$ be a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold of arbitrary complex dimension. If $X$ is special, then $\pi_1(X)$ has an abelian subgroup of finite index. Equivalently, $X$ has a connected finite unramified holomorphic cover with abelian fundamental group.
  - Explanation: A special connected smooth compact Kähler manifold of any dimension has a fundamental group containing an abelian subgroup of finite index. Equivalently it has a finite unramified holomorphic cover with abelian fundamental group.
- **claim-kappa-zero-abelianity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-kappa-zero-abelianity)): Let $X$ be a smooth compact connected Kähler manifold with $\kappa(X)=\kappa(X,K_X)=0$. Then its ordinary topological fundamental group $\pi_1(X)$ is virtually abelian.
  - Explanation: Vanishing Kodaira dimension forces specialness, so the main abelianity theorem applies and the fundamental group is virtually abelian.
- **claim-conjecture-s-iitaka** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-conjecture-s-iitaka)): Let $X$ be a connected compact Kähler manifold of complex dimension $n$. Suppose that at least one of the following holds:* 1. *$X$ is special and $\gamma d(X)=n$;* 2. *the ordinary universal cover of $X$ is biholomorphic to $\mathbb C^n$.* *Then $X$ has a connected finite étale cover $X'\to X$ such that $X'$ is bimeromorphic to a complex torus.
  - Explanation: Under either alternative (special with maximal ordinary Gamma-dimension, or universal cover biholomorphic to complex affine space) a finite étale cover is bimeromorphic to a complex torus.
- **claim-special-holomorphic-convexity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-special-shafarevich)): Let $X$ be a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold. If $X$ is special, then its ordinary universal cover is holomorphically convex.
  - Explanation: A virtually abelian fundamental group has a faithful finite-dimensional linear representation, so the linear Shafarevich theorem gives holomorphic convexity of the universal cover.
- **claim-compactifiable-covers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-compactifiable-covers)): 1. *Let $X$ be a connected normal compact Kähler space and $p:V\to X$ a connected infinite étale Galois covering of complex spaces, with deck group $\Gamma$. If $V$ embeds as a Zariski-open subset of a normal compact complex space, then $\Gamma$ is virtually abelian.* 2. *Let $X$ be a connected compact Kähler manifold and $U$ its ordinary universal cover. If $U$ embeds as a Zariski-open subset of a normal compact Kähler space, then there is a connected finite étale cover $X'\to X$ whose Albanese map $$\alpha_{X'}:X'\longrightarrow A=\mathop{\mathrm{Alb}}(X')$$ is a locally trivial holomorphic fibre bundle with simply connected compact Kähler fibre $F$. Writing $q=\dim_{\mathbb C}A$, there is a biholomorphism $$U\simeq F\times\mathbb C^q.$$ A point is allowed as $A$ or $F$.
  - Explanation: Infinite Galois covers admitting a Zariski-open compactification have virtually abelian deck group; with a Kähler compactification of the universal cover one gets an Albanese bundle on a finite cover and a product description of the universal cover.
- **claim-specialness-inputs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-specialness-inputs)): For smooth compact Kähler manifolds, specialness in the definition of Section <a href="#sec:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:introduction">1</a> is bimeromorphically invariant and is preserved by connected finite étale covers. The Albanese morphism of a special manifold is surjective with connected fibres. Moreover, if a line bundle $L$ has a nonzero morphism to $\Omega_X^p$, then $\kappa(X,L)\le p$.
  - Explanation: Collects the established properties of specialness: bimeromorphic invariance, stability under finite étale covers, surjective Albanese map with connected fibres, and the Iitaka bound for line bundles mapping to differential forms.
- **claim-torus-divisor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-torus-divisor)): Let $D\ne0$ be an effective rational divisor on a compact complex torus $A$. There are a quotient homomorphism $p:A\to B$ with connected fibres, where $B$ is a positive-dimensional abelian variety, and an ample effective rational divisor $D_B$ on $B$ such that $D=p^*D_B$. In particular, $\kappa(A,D)>0$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero effective rational divisor on a compact complex torus is pulled back from an ample divisor on a positive-dimensional abelian quotient, so its Iitaka dimension is positive.
- **claim-gamma-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-gamma-reduction)): Let $M$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold and let $\rho:\pi_1(M)\to P$ be a homomorphism. There is a fibration $\gamma_\rho:M\dashrightarrow B_\rho$ whose general fibre has finite fundamental-group image under $\rho$, and which contracts every compact irreducible subvariety through a very general point whose fundamental group has finite image under $\rho$. The groups of subvarieties may be computed on their smooth resolutions. The base has a compact Kähler model.
  - Explanation: Campana's meromorphic Gamma-reduction attached to a homomorphism of the fundamental group: a fibration with finite-image fibres contracting the relevant subvarieties, with a Kähler base model.
- **claim-linear-general-type** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-linear-general-type)): Suppose a compact Kähler manifold has a generically large complex linear representation with torsion-free image and connected simple semisimple Zariski closure. Then it is of general type.
  - Explanation: A generically large torsion-free linear representation with simple semisimple Zariski closure makes the manifold of general type, via a bimeromorphic Shafarevich morphism.
- **claim-klt-over-tori** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-wang-torus)): Let $Z$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold, let $D$ be an effective rational divisor such that $(Z,D)$ is klt, and let $g:Z\to A$ be a fibration onto a complex torus. For a general smooth fibre $F$, $$\kappa(Z,K_Z+D)\ge\kappa(F,K_F+D\|_F).$$ If $\kappa(Z,K_Z+D)=0$, the Albanese morphism of $Z$ is surjective with connected fibres.
  - Explanation: Subadditivity of log Kodaira dimension for klt pairs fibred over a complex torus, and surjectivity of the Albanese map with connected fibres when the log Kodaira dimension is zero.
- **claim-finite-by-abelian** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-by-abelian)): Let $E$ be a finitely generated group in an exact sequence $$1\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow E\longrightarrow\mathbb Z^r \longrightarrow1$$ with $F$ finite. Then $E$ is virtually abelian and residually finite. In particular it has a finite-index subgroup disjoint from $F$.
  - Explanation: A finitely generated extension of a free abelian group of finite rank by a finite group is virtually abelian and residually finite.
- **claim-realize-finite-index** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-realize-finite-index)): Suppose $G$ has a finitely generated normal subgroup $N$ with $G/N\cong\mathbb Z^r$. For every finite-index subgroup $N_0\le N$, there is a finite-index subgroup $G_0\le G$ satisfying $G_0\cap N\subseteq N_0$.
  - Explanation: Finite-index conditions on the normal fibre subgroup can be realized by passing to a finite-index subgroup of the whole group.
- **claim-zero-spectrum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-zero-spectrum)): Let $(M,\omega)$ be a connected compact Kähler orbifold of complex dimension $n$, and let $U\to M$ be a connected normal orbifold covering with infinite deck group $P$. For some $p\in\{0,\ldots,n\}$, zero belongs to the spectrum of the $L^2$ Dolbeault Laplacian on scalar $(p,0)$-forms on $U$, with the lifted metric.
  - Explanation: An infinite normal cover of a compact Kähler orbifold has zero in the spectrum of the scalar Dolbeault Laplacian in some holomorphic degree.
- **claim-potential-lemma** (construction, [source](#stmt-sp-potential-lemma)): Under <a href="#sp:poincare" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sp:poincare">[sp:poincare]</a>, there are smooth functions $u_N$ on $U$, for all sufficiently large integers $N$, such that $$\label{sp:potential-bounds} u_N\le0,\qquad \mathrm d\mathrm d^cu_N\ge-\omega, \qquad \\|u_N\\|_2\le C N^n.$$ For every compact set $K\subset U$, there are $c_K>0$ and $N_K$ with $$\label{sp:potential-depth} \sup_Ku_N\le-c_KN\qquad(N\ge N_K).$$ For each fixed $N$, the function $u_N$ belongs to every $H^k$ and has bounded derivatives of all orders.
  - Explanation: Under a spectral gap one constructs nonpositive potentials of growing depth on compact sets with controlled global L2 mass and bounded derivatives.
- **claim-cell-lemma** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sp-cell-lemma)): There are $C,\epsilon>0$, independent of $N,a,g$, such that $$\label{sp:cell-count} \#\{g\in P:A_a(g)\ge1\}\le C N^{2n},$$ and $$\label{sp:cell-exponential} \int_{gL}\exp\!\left( \frac{-\epsilon u_N(a^{-1}x)}{A_a(g)+1}\right)\omega^n\le C.$$ For each $R<\infty$ there is $C_R$ such that $$\label{sp:cell-neighbors} C_R^{-1}(A_a(g)+1)\le A_a(h)+1\le C_R(A_a(g)+1)$$ whenever the distance between $gL$ and $hL$ is at most $R$.
  - Explanation: Counts the cells where the potential is deep, gives uniform exponential integrability on cells and comparability of depth between nearby cells.
- **claim-weighted-inverse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sp-weighted-inverse)): Suppose a smooth positive multiplier $h$ on $E^*$ satisfies $$\label{sp:weight-hypotheses} 1\le h\le e^b, \qquad \mathrm i\Theta_{E^*,h}\ge-C_0t\Omega_S, \qquad b,t\ge0.$$ Then the complete $\bar\partial$ complex of $E$-valued $(0,*)$-forms with multiplier $k=h^{-1}$ is exact with closed ranges. Every closed form $F$ in this complex has a solution $\bar\partial v=F$ satisfying $$\label{sp:weighted-solution} \\|v\\|_{S,k}\le C\bigl(e^{b/4}+\sqrt t\,e^{b/2}\bigr)\\|F\\|_{S,k}.$$ The constant depends on $M$, $C_0$ and $\delta$, but not on $S,b,t$.
  - Explanation: A weighted solution operator for the d-bar complex with a quantitative norm bound depending on the weight bounds but not on the metric scaling.
- **claim-smoothing-lemma** (construction, [source](#stmt-sp-smoothing-lemma)): There is a smoothing operator $K$ on the complete Dolbeault complex of $E$-valued $(0,*)$-forms on $D$ with the following properties:* 1. *$K$ commutes with $\bar\partial$ and preserves separately the antiholomorphic degrees in $x$ and $y$.* 2. *Its propagation is at most a fixed $R$, and its kernel and all kernel derivatives are uniformly bounded in the lifted charts.* 3. *For compactly supported currents, $K$ commutes with trace to $M\times M$. On closed such currents it preserves the cohomology class of their trace.* *If $B$ is an $E$-valued $(0,n)$ current with cellwise $L^1$ coefficients, then $$\label{sp:smoothing-norm} \\|KB\\|_S\le C S^{n/2} \left(\sum_C\left(\int_C\|B\|_S\,\mathrm dV_1\right)^2\right)^{1/2},$$ where $C$ runs through the product cells modulo the diagonal action.
  - Explanation: A smoothing operator of fixed propagation commuting with d-bar, with uniformly bounded kernel, preserving the traced cohomology class and satisfying a scaled norm bound.
- **claim-one-step** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sp-one-step)): Let $F$ be a smooth compactly supported closed $E$-valued $(0,n)$-form supported in $\mathcal D_j$. There is a smooth compactly supported closed form $F_{\mathrm{new}}$ of the same type whose traced cohomology class is that of $F$, with $$\label{sp:one-step-norm} \\|F_{\mathrm{new}}\\|_S\le C_jN^{-1/6}\\|F\\|_S.$$ Its support is in the fixed $R$-neighborhood of $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\chi_j$, has standard volume at most $C_jN^{2n}$, and lies in $\mathcal D_{j+1}$ when $j<r$.
  - Explanation: One localization step replaces a closed form supported in the deep region by a cohomologous one of strictly smaller norm and controlled support.
- **claim-hilbert-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-hilbert-positivity)): Let $Y$ be a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $\ell>0$, and let $$\Delta=\sum_i(1-m_i^{-1})D_i,\qquad m_i\in\mathbb N,\quad m_i\ge2,$$ have simple normal crossing support. Denote its root orbifold by $\mathcal Y$. Suppose that $$q:\pi_1^\mathrm{orb}(\mathcal Y)\longrightarrow Q$$ is surjective, that $Q$ is infinite, and that every finite-dimensional complex linear representation of $Q$ has finite image.* *Suppose that a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold $S$ admits a dominant meromorphic map $a:S\dashrightarrow Y$ and a homomorphism $\nu:\pi_1(S)\to Q$ with finite-index image. Assume that $a$ and $\nu$ are compatible with $q$ on a dense analytic Zariski-open subset where $a$ is holomorphic and takes values in $Y\setminus\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}\Delta$.* *Finally, suppose that for every finite-index subgroup $Q_0\le Q$ there is a connected finite étale cover $S'\to S$ such that, writing $\nu'$ for the induced homomorphism and $Q'=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nu'$, one has $Q'\subset Q_0$ and $$\operatorname{gdim}(S',\psi\circ\nu')\ge\ell \quad\text{whenever }\psi:Q'\twoheadrightarrow H,\quad H\text{ is infinite}. \tag{M}\label{hi:minimality}$$ Then $K_Y+\Delta$ is big.
  - Explanation: An infinite quotient of an orbifold fundamental group with only finite linear images, together with a minimality condition on Gamma-dimensions, forces the log canonical divisor of the base to be big.
- **claim-finite-images** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hi-finite-images)): Every finite-index subgroup $Q_0$ of $Q$ has only finite finite-dimensional complex linear images. In particular, $H^1(Q_0,\mathbb C)=0$.
  - Explanation: Finite-index subgroups of Q inherit the property that every finite-dimensional linear image is finite, hence have vanishing first complex cohomology.
- **claim-hilbert-coefficients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hi-coefficients)): There exist an integer $p>0$, a separable unitary representation $\rho:Q\to U(\mathcal H)$ with no nonzero finite-dimensional invariant subspace, and a nonzero holomorphic section $$\eta\in H^0\bigl(\mathcal Y,\Omega_{\mathcal Y}^p\otimes\mathcal H_\rho\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Existence of a nonzero holomorphic form with unitary Hilbert coefficients and no finite-dimensional invariant subspace, from regular-representation ultrapowers in the nonamenable case and reduced cohomology in the amenable case.
- **claim-plucker-line-tensor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hi-plucker)): There is a line tensor whose coefficient lines have infinite-dimensional closed span.
  - Explanation: Exterior powers produce a rank-one line tensor whose coefficient lines span an infinite-dimensional space.
- **claim-saturated-line** (construction, [source](#stmt-hi-saturated-line)): A line tensor determines a saturated orbifold line subsheaf $$\mathcal L\subset(\Omega_{\mathcal Y_0}^1)^{\otimes m}$$ and a positive curvature current on $\mathcal L$. On the regular locus this current is the pullback of the Fubini–Study form of its coefficient-line map.
  - Explanation: A line tensor saturates to an orbifold line subsheaf of a cotangent tensor power carrying a positive curvature current given by the pulled-back Fubini–Study form.
- **claim-discrete-monodromy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hi-discrete-monodromy)): After replacing $\Gamma$ by a finite-index subgroup, the action on the regular image germs of $F$ has infinite discrete image in a Lie group acting properly on a connected complex manifold of dimension $s$.
  - Explanation: For a minimal-rank line tensor, the monodromy acts on the manifold of image germs through an infinite discrete group acting properly.
- **claim-compact-fibres** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hi-compact-fibres)): Let $V$ be a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $d$, and let $V^\circ$ be the complement of a proper analytic subset. Let a discrete group $I$ act properly and effectively by holomorphic isometries on a connected Kähler manifold $Z$ of dimension $s>0$. Suppose that $f:V^\circ\to Z/I$ has local holomorphic lifts to $Z$ of rank $s$, whose continuation is compatible with a homomorphism $\rho:\pi_1(V)\to I$. If a positive closed $(1,1)$-current on $V$ restricts to a positive constant multiple of $f^*\omega_Z$, then $$\operatorname{gdim}(V,\rho)\le s. \label{hi:gamma-upper-bound}$$
  - Explanation: A positive current restricting to a multiple of the pulled-back Kähler form yields compact fibres with finite group image, bounding the Gamma-dimension by the target dimension.
- **claim-inertia-divisibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-de-inertia-divisibility)): Let $f:X\to Z$ be a dominant holomorphic map of connected smooth complex manifolds. Suppose that a homomorphism $\rho:\pi_1(X)\to R$ is compatible, over a dense analytic Zariski open $Z^\circ$, with a homomorphism $\psi:\pi_1(Z^\circ)\to R$. Let $p:Z\to W$ be a smooth proper surjective map with connected fibres, with $W$ smooth. Assume that the nontrivial divisorial meridian orders of $\psi$ give the boundary $$\Delta_Z=p^*\Delta_W, \qquad \Delta_W=\sum_D(1-m_D^{-1})D, \qquad m_D\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}.$$ Thus every other divisorial meridian has trivial image. If a prime divisor $E\subset X$ dominates $D\subset W$ under $p\circ f$, then $$\label{de:all-component-divisibility} m_D\mid \operatorname{ord}_E\bigl((p\circ f)^*D\bigr).$$ This includes the case in which $f(E)$ is a proper subvariety of $p^{-1}(D)$.
  - Explanation: The meridian order of a base divisor divides the multiplicity of every source divisor dominating it, including those with small image.
- **claim-small-boundary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-de-small-boundary)): Let $u:X\to B$ be a surjective holomorphic map from a smooth compact Kähler manifold to a positive-dimensional complex torus. Let $\Delta=\sum_D(1-m_D^{-1})D$ be an ample rational divisor on $B$, with integers $m_D\ge2$. Suppose that $$m_D\mid\operatorname{ord}_E(u^*D)$$ for every prime divisor $E$ dominating $D$ under $u$. Then $X$ is not special.
  - Explanation: An ample orbifold boundary on a torus compatible with multiplicities of pullback divisors produces a Bogomolov sheaf, so the source is not special.
- **claim-neat-pullback** (theorem, [source](#stmt-de-neat-pullback)): Let $u:\widehat X\to W$ and $\nu:\widehat X\to X$ be as above, with $X$ smooth compact Kähler and $W$ smooth of dimension $r>0$. Let $\Delta_W=\sum_D(1-m_D^{-1})D$, with $m_D\ge2$. Suppose that $K_W+\Delta_W$ is big and that $m_D$ divides $\operatorname{ord}_E(u^*D)$ for every component $E$ dominating $D$. Then $X$ is not special.
  - Explanation: On a neat model, bigness of the log canonical divisor with divisible multiplicities gives a Bogomolov sheaf on the source.
- **claim-birational-pushdown** (theorem, [source](#stmt-de-birational-pushdown)): Let $\mu:Z'\to Z$ be a modification between smooth base models of the same fibration and quotient. For sufficiently divisible $m$, there is a natural injection $$H^0\bigl(Z',m(K_{Z'}+\Delta_{Z'})\bigr) \hookrightarrow H^0\bigl(Z,m(K_Z+\Delta_Z)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Log pluricanonical sections on a higher model push down injectively to a lower model of the same fibration and quotient.
- **claim-common-fibres** (theorem, [source](#stmt-de-common-fibres)): Let $g:Z\to A$ and $h:Z\to W$ be surjective holomorphic maps with connected general fibres between compact complex manifolds, with $Z$ smooth. Let $L$ be a rational line bundle whose restriction to a very general smooth $g$-fibre is big. Then $L$ restricts big to every component of a general fibre of the map $$(h,g):Z\longrightarrow J\subseteq W\times A,$$ where $J$ denotes its image. Equivalently, one may first take a very general $h$-fibre $H$, and then a general fibre of $g\|_H$ over its image. No equality $g(H)=A$ is required.
  - Explanation: Bigness on very general fibres of one map persists on general components of common fibres with a second map.
- **claim-torus-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-torus-descent)): Let $M$ be a special smooth compact Kähler manifold, and let $\alpha:M\to A$ be a surjective map to a complex torus with connected fibres. Suppose that $$M\dashrightarrow Z\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}A$$ is a factorization by fibrations, and that $d=\dim Z-\dim A>0$. On every prepared model of $Z$, take the inertial boundary for $$R=\pi_1(M)/\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\bigl(\pi_1(F_c)\to\pi_1(M)\bigr),$$ where $F_c$ is a smooth general fibre of $M\dashrightarrow Z$. It is impossible that, on a cofinal collection of prepared models, $K_Y+\Delta_Z\|_Y$ is big for a very general $g$-fibre $Y$. The torus $A$ is allowed to be a point.
  - Explanation: On a special manifold, fibrewise bigness of the inertial log canonical divisor over a torus on a cofinal family of prepared models is impossible.
- **claim-albanese-exactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-albanese-exactness)): The natural sequence is exact: $$1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow G \xrightarrow{\alpha_*}\pi_1(A)\longrightarrow1.$$
  - Explanation: The Albanese fibre-group image sits in a short exact sequence with the total fundamental group and the fundamental group of the Albanese torus.
- **claim-fibre-fab** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-fibre-fab)): The group $N$ is FAb.
  - Explanation: Every finite-index subgroup of the Albanese fibre group has finite abelianization, using vanishing of twisted cohomology and torsion of isolated jump-locus points.
- **claim-minimal-base** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-minimal-base)): After a further connected finite étale cover, there is a fibration $c:X\dashrightarrow Z$ over the Albanese torus $A$, with connected general fibres and relative base dimension $\ell$. If $B_0$ is the actual image of a general $c$-fibre group, then $B_0\subseteq N$, $B_0\triangleleft G$, and $Q=N/B_0$ is infinite.* *For each prepared smooth model of $Z$, a very general good point of $A$ gives an Albanese fibre $S$ and a torus fibre $Y$. With the restricted actual inertial boundary $\Delta_Y$ for $R=G/B_0$, there is a surjection $$\pi_1^\mathrm{orb}(Y,\Delta_Y)\twoheadrightarrow Q$$ compatible with $S\dashrightarrow Y$ and the natural $\nu:\pi_1(S)\twoheadrightarrow Q$. For every finite-index subgroup $Q_0\le Q$, there is a connected finite étale cover $S'\to S$ with induced homomorphism $\nu'$ whose image $Q'=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nu'$ lies in $Q_0$. This one cover satisfies $$\operatorname{gdim}(S',\psi\circ\nu')\ge\ell$$ for every surjection $\psi:Q'\twoheadrightarrow P$ with $P$ infinite.
  - Explanation: Constructs, after a finite cover, a fibration over the Albanese torus of minimal relative base dimension with an infinite quotient, the orbifold surjection onto it, and the uniform minimality inequality for Gamma-dimensions.

      "exact_statement": "For smooth compact Kähler manifolds, specialness in the definition of Section <a href=\"#sec:introduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:introduction\">1</a> is bimeromorphically invariant and is preserved by connected finite étale covers.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-torus-divisor",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D\\ne0$ be an effective rational divisor on a compact complex torus $A$. There are a quotient homomorphism $p:A\\to B$ with connected fibres, where $B$ is a positive-dimensional abelian variety, and an ample effective rational divisor $D_B$ on $B$ such that $D=p^*D_B$.         "source_id": "PM-057.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-gamma-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a smooth compact Kähler manifold and let $\\rho:\\pi_1(M)\\to P$ be a homomorphism.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sp-potential-lemma",
        "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#sp:poincare\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sp:poincare\">[sp:poincare]</a>, there are smooth functions $u_N$ on $U$, for all sufficiently large integers $N$, such that $$\\label{sp:potential-bounds}\n u_N\\le0,\\qquad \\mathrm d\\mathrm d^cu_N\\ge-\\omega,\n \\qquad \\|u_N\\|_2\\le C N^n.$$ For every compact set $K\\subset U$, there are $c_K>0$ and $N_K$ with $$\\label{sp:potential-depth}\n \\sup_Ku_N\\le-c_KN\\qquad(N\\ge N_K).$$ For each fixed $N$, the function $u_N$ belongs to every $H^k$ and has bounded derivatives of all orders.",
        "source_id": "PM-057.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sp-potential-lemma",
          "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#sp:poincare\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sp:poincare\">[sp:poincare]</a>, there are smooth functions $u_N$ on $U$, for all sufficiently large integers $N$, such that $$\\label{sp:potential-bounds}\n u_N\\le0,\\qquad \\mathrm d\\mathrm d^cu_N\\ge-\\omega,\n \\qquad \\|u_N\\|_2\\le C N^n.$$ For every compact set $K\\subset U$, there are $c_K>0$ and $N_K$ with $$\\label{sp:potential-depth}\n \\sup_Ku_N\\le-c_KN\\qquad(N\\ge N_K).$$ For each fixed $N$, the function $u_N$ belongs to every $H^k$ and has bounded derivatives of all orders.",
          "source_id": "PM-057.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under <a href=\"#sp:poincare\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"sp:poincare\">[sp:poincare]</a>, there are smooth functions $u_N$ on $U$, for all sufficiently large integers $N$, such that $$\\label{sp:potential-bounds}\n u_N\\le0,\\qquad \\mathrm d\\mathrm d^cu_N\\ge-\\omega,\n \\qquad \\|u_N\\|_2\\le C N^n.$$ For every compact set $K\\subset U$, there are $c_K>0$ and $N_K$ with $$\\label{sp:potential-depth}\n \\sup_Ku_N\\le-c_KN\\qquad(N\\ge N_K).$$ For each fixed $N$, the function $u_N$ belongs to every $H^k$ and has bounded derivatives of all orders.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sp-cell-lemma",
        "exact_quote": "There are $C,\\epsilon>0$, independent of $N,a,g$, such that $$\\label{sp:cell-count}\n \\#\\{g\\in P:A_a(g)\\ge1\\}\\le C N^{2n},$$ and $$\\label{sp:cell-exponential}\n \\int_{gL}\\exp\\!\\left(\n       \\frac{-\\epsilon u_N(a^{-1}x)}{A_a(g)+1}\\right)\\omega^n\\le C.$$ For each $R<\\infty$ there is $C_R$ such that $$\\label{sp:cell-neighbors}\n C_R^{-1}(A_a(g)+1)\\le A_a(h)+1\\le C_R(A_a(g)+1)$$ whenever the distance between $gL$ and $hL$ is at most $R$.",
        "source_id": "PM-057.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-sp-weighted-inverse",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a smooth positive multiplier $h$ on $E^*$ satisfies $$\\label{sp:weight-hypotheses}\n 1\\le h\\le e^b,\n \\qquad \\mathrm i\\Theta_{E^*,h}\\ge-C_0t\\Omega_S,\n \\qquad b,t\\ge0.$$ Then the complete $\\bar\\partial$ complex of $E$-valued $(0,*)$-forms with multiplier $k=h^{-1}$ is exact with closed ranges. Every closed form $F$ in this complex has a solution $\\bar\\partial v=F$ satisfying $$\\label{sp:weighted-solution}\n \\|v\\|_{S,k}\\le\n C\\bigl(e^{b/4}+\\sqrt t\\,e^{b/2}\\bigr)\\|F\\|_{S,k}.$$ The constant depends on $M$, $C_0$ and $\\delta$, but not on $S,b,t$.",

Every closed form $F$ in this complex has a solution $\\bar\\partial v=F$ satisfying $$\\label{sp:weighted-solution}\n \\|v\\|_{S,k}\\le\n C\\bigl(e^{b/4}+\\sqrt t\\,e^{b/2}\\bigr)\\|F\\|_{S,k}.$$ The constant depends on $M$, $C_0$ and $\\delta$, but not on $S,b,t$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sp-smoothing-lemma",
        "exact_quote": "There is a smoothing operator $K$ on the complete Dolbeault complex of $E$-valued $(0,*)$-forms on $D$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$K$ commutes with $\\bar\\partial$ and preserves separately the antiholomorphic degrees in $x$ and $y$.*\n\n2.  *Its propagation is at most a fixed $R$, and its kernel and all kernel derivatives are uniformly bounded in the lifted charts.*\n\n3.  *For compactly supported currents, $K$ commutes with trace to $M\\times M$. On closed such currents it preserves the cohomology class of their trace.*\n\n\n*If $B$ is an $E$-valued $(0,n)$ current with cellwise $L^1$ coefficients, then $$\\label{sp:smoothing-norm}\n \\|KB\\|_S\\le C S^{n/2}\n \\left(\\sum_C\\left(\\int_C|B|_S\\,\\mathrm dV_1\\right)^2\\right)^{1/2},$$ where $C$ runs through the product cells modulo the diagonal action.",
        "source_id": "PM-057.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sp-smoothing-lemma",
          "exact_quote": "There is a smoothing operator $K$ on the complete Dolbeault complex of $E$-valued $(0,*)$-forms on $D$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$K$ commutes with $\\bar\\partial$ and preserves separately the antiholomorphic degrees in $x$ and $y$.*\n\n2.  *Its propagation is at most a fixed $R$, and its kernel and all kernel derivatives are uniformly bounded in the lifted charts.*\n\n3.  *For compactly supported currents, $K$ commutes with trace to $M\\times M$. On closed such currents it preserves the cohomology class of their trace.*\n\n\n*If $B$ is an $E$-valued $(0,n)$ current with cellwise $L^1$ coefficients, then $$\\label{sp:smoothing-norm}\n \\|KB\\|_S\\le C S^{n/2}\n \\left(\\sum_C\\left(\\int_C|B|_S\\,\\mathrm dV_1\\right)^2\\right)^{1/2},$$ where $C$ runs through the product cells modulo the diagonal action.",
          "source_id": "PM-057.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a smoothing operator $K$ on the complete Dolbeault complex of $E$-valued $(0,*)$-forms on $D$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$K$ commutes with $\\bar\\partial$ and preserves separately the antiholomorphic degrees in $x$ and $y$.*\n\n2.  *Its propagation is at most a fixed $R$, and its kernel and all kernel derivatives are uniformly bounded in the lifted charts.*\n\n3.  *For compactly supported currents, $K$ commutes with trace to $M\\times M$.         "anchor": "stmt-sp-one-step",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a smooth compactly supported closed $E$-valued $(0,n)$-form supported in $\\mathcal D_j$.         "source_id": "PM-057.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-hilbert-positivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $\\ell>0$, and let $$\\Delta=\\sum_i(1-m_i^{-1})D_i,\\qquad m_i\\in\\mathbb N,\\quad m_i\\ge2,$$ have simple normal crossing support. Denote its root orbifold by $\\mathcal Y$. Suppose that $$q:\\pi_1^\\mathrm{orb}(\\mathcal Y)\\longrightarrow Q$$ is surjective, that $Q$ is infinite, and that every finite-dimensional complex linear representation of $Q$ has finite image.*\n\n*Suppose that a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold $S$ admits a dominant meromorphic map $a:S\\dashrightarrow Y$ and a homomorphism $\\nu:\\pi_1(S)\\to Q$ with finite-index image. Assume that $a$ and $\\nu$ are compatible with $q$ on a dense analytic Zariski-open subset where $a$ is holomorphic and takes values in $Y\\setminus\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}\\Delta$.*\n\n\n*Finally, suppose that for every finite-index subgroup $Q_0\\le Q$ there is a connected finite étale cover $S'\\to S$ such that, writing $\\nu'$ for the induced homomorphism and $Q'=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}\\nu'$, one has $Q'\\subset Q_0$ and $$\\operatorname{gdim}(S',\\psi\\circ\\nu')\\ge\\ell\n \\quad\\text{whenever }\\psi:Q'\\twoheadrightarrow H,\\quad\n H\\text{ is infinite}.\n \\tag{M}\\label{hi:minimality}$$ Then $K_Y+\\Delta$ is big.",
        "source_id": "PM-057.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Assume that $a$ and $\\nu$ are compatible with $q$ on a dense analytic Zariski-open subset where $a$ is holomorphic and takes values in $Y\\setminus\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}\\Delta$.*\n\n\n*Finally, suppose that for every finite-index subgroup $Q_0\\le Q$ there is a connected finite étale cover $S'\\to S$ such that, writing $\\nu'$ for the induced homomorphism and $Q'=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Im}}\\nu'$, one has $Q'\\subset Q_0$ and $$\\operatorname{gdim}(S',\\psi\\circ\\nu')\\ge\\ell\n \\quad\\text{whenever }\\psi:Q'\\twoheadrightarrow H,\\quad\n H\\text{ is infinite}.\n \\tag{M}\\label{hi:minimality}$$ Then $K_Y+\\Delta$ is big.",
          "source_id": "PM-057.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-hi-compact-fibres",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ be a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold of dimension $d$, and let $V^\\circ$ be the complement of a proper analytic subset. Let a discrete group $I$ act properly and effectively by holomorphic isometries on a connected Kähler manifold $Z$ of dimension $s>0$. Suppose that $f:V^\\circ\\to Z/I$ has local holomorphic lifts to $Z$ of rank $s$, whose continuation is compatible with a homomorphism $\\rho:\\pi_1(V)\\to I$. If a positive closed $(1,1)$-current on $V$ restricts to a positive constant multiple of $f^*\\omega_Z$, then $$\\operatorname{gdim}(V,\\rho)\\le s.\n \\label{hi:gamma-upper-bound}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-057.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-057.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-de-inertia-divisibility",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a dominant holomorphic map of connected smooth complex manifolds. Suppose that a homomorphism $\\rho:\\pi_1(X)\\to R$ is compatible, over a dense analytic Zariski open $Z^\\circ$, with a homomorphism $\\psi:\\pi_1(Z^\\circ)\\to R$. Let $p:Z\\to W$ be a smooth proper surjective map with connected fibres, with $W$ smooth. Assume that the nontrivial divisorial meridian orders of $\\psi$ give the boundary $$\\Delta_Z=p^*\\Delta_W,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta_W=\\sum_D(1-m_D^{-1})D,\n \\qquad m_D\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge2}.$$ Thus every other divisorial meridian has trivial image. If a prime divisor $E\\subset X$ dominates $D\\subset W$ under $p\\circ f$, then $$\\label{de:all-component-divisibility}\n m_D\\mid \\operatorname{ord}_E\\bigl((p\\circ f)^*D\\bigr).$$ This includes the case in which $f(E)$ is a proper subvariety of $p^{-1}(D)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-057.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-de-inertia-divisibility",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a dominant holomorphic map of connected smooth complex manifolds. Let $p:Z\\to W$ be a smooth proper surjective map with connected fibres, with $W$ smooth. Assume that the nontrivial divisorial meridian orders of $\\psi$ give the boundary $$\\Delta_Z=p^*\\Delta_W,\n \\qquad\n \\Delta_W=\\sum_D(1-m_D^{-1})D,\n \\qquad m_D\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\ge2}.$$ Thus every other divisorial meridian has trivial image. If a prime divisor $E\\subset X$ dominates $D\\subset W$ under $p\\circ f$, then $$\\label{de:all-component-divisibility}\n m_D\\mid \\operatorname{ord}_E\\bigl((p\\circ f)^*D\\bigr).$$ This includes the case in which $f(E)$ is a proper subvariety of $p^{-1}(D)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-057.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $f:X\\to Z$ be a dominant holomorphic map of connected smooth complex manifolds. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Bogomolov sheaf** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A line bundle with such a morphism and equality is a *Bogomolov sheaf*.
- **holomorphically convex** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Recall that a complex manifold is *holomorphically convex* if the holomorphic hull of every nonempty compact subset is compact.
- **Zariski-open embedding** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Here a *Zariski-open* embedding is holomorphic and has closed analytic complement.
- **inertial boundary and prepared model** ([source](#stmt-de-inertial-boundary)): The *inertial boundary* associated with $\rho$ on this model of $Z$ is $$\Delta_Z=\sum_D(1-m_D^{-1})D.$$ A *prepared model* is a smooth compact Kähler base model, together with a resolved source, on which the divisorial deleted locus has simple normal crossings. If a map to a fixed torus is present, the model is required to map holomorphically to that torus.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-potential-l2-bound | L2 norm of potential u_N | ≤ | $C N^n$ | All sufficiently large N |
| q-deep-cell-count | number of cells with depth at least one | ≤ | $C N^{2n}$ | C independent of N, a, g |
| q-one-step-contraction | norm contraction in one localization step | ≤ | $C_jN^{-1/6}$ | Fixed step j, N large |
| q-localization-steps | number of localization steps | > | $30n+6$ | Chosen before N tends to infinity |
| q-iitaka-bound | Iitaka dimension of a line bundle mapping to the p-th cotangent power | ≤ | $p$ | Nonzero morphism to the p-th exterior power of the cotangent sheaf |
| q-minimal-base-dimension | minimal base dimension ell | ≤ | $\dim S$ | Infinite fibre group |
| q-relative-base-dimension | dimension of the base Z | ≤ | $\dim A+\ell$ | Cycles of the stated dimension contracted through very general points |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-kappa-zero** [claim](#stmt-cor-kappa-zero-abelianity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-kappa-zero-abelianity): Specialness of Kodaira dimension zero manifolds, then the main theorem. -> virtual abelianity in Kodaira dimension zero
- **step-shafarevich** [claim](#stmt-cor-special-shafarevich) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-special-shafarevich): Induce a faithful linear representation from a finite-index abelian subgroup and apply the linear Shafarevich theorem. -> holomorphic convexity of the universal cover
- **step-conjecture-s** [claim](#stmt-cor-conjecture-s-iitaka) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-conjecture-s-iitaka): Torsion-free abelian finite cover, Albanese map isomorphism on fundamental groups, maximal Gamma-dimension forces a point fibre. -> finite cover bimeromorphic to a torus
- **step-compactifiable** [claim](#stmt-cor-compactifiable-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-compactifiable-covers): Minimal counterexample reduction to a special base, then the main theorem. -> virtually abelian deck group
- **step-finite-by-abelian** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-by-abelian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-by-abelian): Centralizer of the finite kernel has finite index; commutators are central; pass to powers of generators. -> virtual abelianity and residual finiteness
- **step-realize-finite-index** [claim](#stmt-lem-realize-finite-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-realize-finite-index): Characteristic core of the subgroup, then the finite-by-abelian lemma. -> finite-index subgroup of G meeting N inside N_0
- **step-albanese-exactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-albanese-exactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-albanese-exactness): Show all base meridians are trivial in the quotient, otherwise a divisor on the torus yields a Bogomolov sheaf. -> exact sequence for the Albanese fibre group
- **step-inertia-small-boundary** [claim](#stmt-de-small-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-de-small-boundary): Compare vanishing orders of top differentials with boundary poles; build sections of the saturated differential line. -> contradiction with specialness
- **step-neat-pullback** [claim](#stmt-de-neat-pullback) / [proof](#proof-stmt-de-neat-pullback): Pull back log pluricanonical sections to the saturated differential line using the cancellation inequality. -> Bogomolov sheaf on the source
- **step-fibre-fab** [claim](#stmt-prop-fibre-fab) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fibre-fab): Inflation–restriction, isolated jump-locus points are torsion, isogeny reduction, vanishing transgression. -> finite abelianization of finite-index subgroups of N
- **step-spectral-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-thm-zero-spectrum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-one-step-2): Assume a spectral gap; iterate the localization step to drive the pairing with the diagonal class to zero against a positive constant. -> zero lies in the spectrum in some degree
- **step-one-step** [claim](#stmt-sp-one-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sp-one-step): Solve d-bar with the weighted inverse, cut off, then apply the smoothing operator. -> cohomologous form with smaller norm
- **step-hilbert-coefficients** [claim](#stmt-hi-coefficients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hi-coefficients): Nonamenable case: spectral theorem plus Hilbert ultrapower; amenable case: Shalom reduced cohomology and harmonic projection. -> holomorphic form with unitary Hilbert coefficients
- **step-discrete-monodromy** [claim](#stmt-hi-discrete-monodromy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hi-discrete-monodromy): Manifold of image germs, isometry Lie group, spectral decomposition, minimality of rank. -> discrete proper monodromy action
- **step-hilbert-positivity** [claim](#stmt-thm-hilbert-positivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hi-compact-fibres-2): Rank must equal the base dimension by minimality; tensor product of translates gives a big line bundle; projectivity and orbifold cotangent positivity give bigness. -> K_Y plus boundary is big
- **step-torus-descent** [claim](#stmt-thm-torus-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-torus-descent): Minimal Iitaka model, torus bundle model, descent of bigness, neat model and Bogomolov sheaf. -> contradiction with specialness
- **step-minimal-base** [claim](#stmt-prop-minimal-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-minimal-base): Normalize the fibre group, apply Gamma-reduction, restrict inertia to a general fibre, choose covers for minimality. -> minimal relative base with infinite quotient
- **step-final-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-abelianity) / [proof](#pm-h-40): Split into finite linear images (Hilbert tensors) and an infinite linear image (general type via Selberg), both contradicting specialness through torus descent. -> N finite, hence the group is virtually abelian

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-special-virtually-abelian**: For a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold, specialness forces the topological fundamental group to contain an abelian subgroup of finite index; the finite index may depend on the manifold.
- **inv-kodaira-zero-virtually-abelian**: Vanishing Kodaira dimension of a smooth compact connected Kähler manifold implies, via specialness, that its ordinary fundamental group is virtually abelian.
- **inv-albanese-fibre-group-fab**: The image of the fundamental group of a general Albanese fibre is a group all of whose finite-index subgroups have finite abelianization.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-non-special-manifold**: Conclude virtual abelianity of the fundamental group of a compact Kähler manifold without checking specialness. -> The conclusion is stated only under the specialness hypothesis; manifolds carrying a Bogomolov sheaf are outside the theorem, and the paper itself notes a surface that is not special.
- **anti-non-kahler-or-noncompact**: Apply the theorem to a complex manifold that is non-Kähler or non-compact. -> The proof uses Kähler analysis on compact orbifolds and compactness of the Albanese setup; outside this setting the statement is not asserted.
- **anti-computational-use**: Use the theorem as an algorithm or a computable bound for finding the abelian finite-index subgroup or its index. -> The result is an existence statement proved by contradiction; it supplies no procedure and no explicit index.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are existence statements about fundamental groups of compact Kähler manifolds, proved by contradiction through minimal fibrations, spectral analysis on infinite covers and positivity of canonical divisors. The statement says the group has an abelian subgroup of finite index, with no finite procedure that computes this subgroup or its index, so there is no finite constructive object to implement and test.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves structural statements about fundamental groups of compact Kähler manifolds. It supplies no finite procedure that a program could execute, so software gets only a precondition-checking role. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No discrete dynamics, state space or simulable model appears. The objects are complex analytic manifolds and their covers, which no game or simulation consumes. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A tool could at most use the theorem as a documented classification fact: if a manifold is known to be special or of Kodaira dimension zero, its fundamental group is virtually abelian. Specialness itself is not decidable by any procedure in the text, so no tool contract is constructible. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof is a contradiction argument built on minimal base dimension, spectral analysis and positivity of line bundles. It contains no finite constructive procedure with explicit steps, so no complexity bound is meaningful. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper defines no data layout or representation. The groups involved are infinite and appear only through abstract exact sequences. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no numerical kernel or fixed-size computation. The analytic estimates (potentials, weighted inverses, smoothing operators) are proof devices on noncompact covers, not implementable arithmetic. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result classifies topological fundamental groups of complex manifolds. It yields no hardness assumption, no primitive, and no security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Unitary Hilbert-space representations and holomorphic tensors appear only as proof tools for group quotients. They involve no learning problem, data, or loss. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only extremal step is choosing a minimal base dimension, a minimum over a finite set of integers inside a proof. No objective, constraint set or solver is involved. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No concurrency, communication or replication model is present. Deck group actions on covers are geometric symmetries, not distributed protocols. |
| other | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The real use is as a mathematical prerequisite and classification input in complex geometry. A downstream result can cite it to restrict fundamental groups of special or Kodaira-dimension-zero compact Kähler manifolds, to obtain holomorphic convexity of universal covers, and to obtain torus uniformization. It is a theorem to cite under its hypotheses, not a construction. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-special-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The abelianity conclusion is asserted only when the manifold is special, that is, when no line bundle with a nonzero morphism to a cotangent exterior power attains Iitaka dimension equal to that power. Blocked use: ['Concluding virtual abelianity for a compact Kähler manifold whose specialness has not been established', 'Applying the theorem to manifolds carrying a Bogomolov sheaf']
- **lim-kahler-compact** (hypothesis): The theorem concerns connected smooth compact Kähler manifolds; the spectral and Hodge-type arguments use the Kähler structure and compactness. Blocked use: ['Applying the result to non-Kähler compact complex manifolds', 'Applying the result to non-compact or quasi-projective varieties']
- **lim-finite-index-nonuniform** (edge_case): The finite index of the abelian subgroup may depend on the manifold, and torsion in the fundamental group is allowed, so the group itself need not be abelian or torsion-free. Blocked use: ['Assuming the fundamental group itself is abelian', 'Assuming a uniform bound on the index across manifolds']
- **lim-kodaira-zero** (hypothesis): The Kodaira dimension corollary requires the canonical bundle to have Kodaira dimension exactly zero, which is used only to reach specialness. Blocked use: ['Using the corollary for manifolds of positive or negative Kodaira dimension']
- **lim-holomorphic-convexity-not-stein** (edge_case): The universal cover of a special manifold is holomorphically convex but need not be Stein; the projective line is special with compact universal cover. Blocked use: ['Inferring Steinness of the universal cover from specialness']
- **lim-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The proof argues by contradiction from an infinite Albanese fibre group and uses ultrafilters, so it gives no procedure and no explicit index. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem as an algorithm to compute the finite-index abelian subgroup', 'Extracting an effective bound on the index']
- **lim-spectral-gap-assumption** (formal_scope): The potential, cell, weighted-inverse and smoothing estimates are proved under an assumed spectral gap and serve only to derive a contradiction; they are not unconditional statements about covers. Blocked use: ['Citing these estimates as unconditional results on infinite covers']
- **lim-minimality-condition** (hypothesis): The Hilbert positivity theorem needs the minimality condition on Gamma-dimensions for every infinite quotient, together with an infinite quotient having only finite linear images. Blocked use: ['Concluding bigness of the log canonical divisor without the minimality condition']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the fundamental group of every special compact Kähler manifold is virtually abelian, resolving Campana's abelianity conjecture in all dimensions. In particular, every smooth compact connected Kähler manifold of Kodaira dimension zero has virtually abelian ordinary fundamental group.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Campana’s classification program separates compact Kähler geometry into special varieties and bases of general type. Specialness records the absence of differential forms that would detect such a base. One of the program’s central predictions is topological: the fundamental group of a special compact Kähler manifold should become abelian after passage to a finite unramified cover (Claudon and Höring 2013, Conjecture A.1) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)].

We first specify the notion of specialness. For a holomorphic line bundle $L$ on a compact complex manifold $X$, its Iitaka dimension $\kappa(X,L)$ is $-\infty$ if no positive tensor power has a nonzero section, and otherwise is the largest dimension of the images of the meromorphic maps defined by its complete plurisection systems. A manifold $X$ of dimension $n$ is *special* if there is no integer $1\le p\le n$ and no nonzero sheaf morphism $$L\longrightarrow\Omega_X^p
 \qquad\text{with}\qquad \kappa(X,L)=p.$$ A line bundle with such a morphism and equality is a *Bogomolov sheaf*. A point is special. We prove the following form of Campana’s abelianity conjecture.

<a id="stmt-thm-abelianity"></a>
<div id="thm:abelianity" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $X$ be a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold of arbitrary complex dimension. If $X$ is special, then $\pi_1(X)$ has an abelian subgroup of finite index. Equivalently, $X$ has a connected finite unramified holomorphic cover with abelian fundamental group.*

</div>

This is a positive resolution of the conjecture in the compact Kähler setting. The conclusion concerns the entire discrete topological fundamental group. It requires no linearity, residual finiteness, projectivity, or prescribed Kodaira dimension. The finite index may depend on $X$, and torsion in $\pi_1(X)$ is allowed.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Kodaira dimension zero

Campana’s Theorem 6.7, applied to the zero-boundary orbifold $(X|0)$, shows that a smooth connected compact Kähler manifold of Kodaira dimension zero is special (Campana 2011, Theorem 6.7, p. 92) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[CampanaOrbifolds](#ref-CampanaOrbifolds)]. Combining this implication with Theorem <a href="#thm:abelianity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abelianity">1</a> gives the following consequence.

<a id="stmt-cor-kappa-zero-abelianity"></a>
<div id="cor:kappa-zero-abelianity" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Fundamental groups in Kodaira dimension zero). *Let $X$ be a smooth compact connected Kähler manifold with $\kappa(X)=\kappa(X,K_X)=0$. Then its ordinary topological fundamental group $\pi_1(X)$ is virtually abelian.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-kappa-zero-abelianity"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-kappa-zero-abelianity</summary>

*Proof.* By Campana’s established specialness implication, $X$ is special. Theorem <a href="#thm:abelianity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abelianity">1</a> therefore applies. ◻

</details>

The specialness implication is also recorded in the orbifold core formulation of (OpenAI 2026b, Corollary *Kodaira dimension along the core*) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[OpenAIIitaka2026](#ref-OpenAIIitaka2026)], where its established provenance is noted. No projectivity, vanishing of $c_1(X)$, linearity, or residual-finiteness assumption is required for Corollary <a href="#cor:kappa-zero-abelianity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:kappa-zero-abelianity">2</a>.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Conjecture S and affine-space uniformization

We also record two geometric consequences identified by Campana. For a connected compact Kähler manifold $X$, let $\gamma_X:X\dashrightarrow\Gamma(X)$ be its ordinary $\Gamma$-reduction. An irreducible compact analytic subspace $Z$ through a very general point is contained in the corresponding fibre precisely when $\pi_1(\widehat Z)\to\pi_1(X)$ has finite image, where $\widehat Z$ is the normalization of $Z$. Set $\gamma d(X)=\dim\Gamma(X)$. This is the reduction for the identity quotient of $\pi_1(X)$, and maximal ordinary $\Gamma$-dimension means $\gamma d(X)=\dim X$ (Claudon and Höring 2013, Definition 2.2 and the paragraph preceding Conjecture A.3) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)]. The two cases below are, respectively, Campana’s Conjecture S and the bimeromorphic formulation of Iitaka’s conjecture in his appendix (Claudon and Höring 2013, Conjecture A.3 and Remark A.4(2)) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)].

<a id="stmt-cor-conjecture-s-iitaka"></a>
<div id="cor:conjecture-s-iitaka" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Conjecture S and bimeromorphic Iitaka uniformization). *Let $X$ be a connected compact Kähler manifold of complex dimension $n$. Suppose that at least one of the following holds:*

1.  *$X$ is special and $\gamma d(X)=n$;*

2.  *the ordinary universal cover of $X$ is biholomorphic to $\mathbb C^n$.*

*Then $X$ has a connected finite étale cover $X'\to X$ such that $X'$ is bimeromorphic to a complex torus.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-conjecture-s-iitaka"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-conjecture-s-iitaka</summary>

*Proof.* Theorem <a href="#thm:abelianity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abelianity">1</a> supplies the Abelianity Conjecture. Campana proves that the Abelianity Conjecture implies Conjecture S in (Claudon and Höring 2013, Remark A.4(3)) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)]. We spell out the finite-cover step. A finitely generated virtually abelian group has a torsion-free abelian subgroup of finite index, so take the corresponding connected finite étale cover $X'$. Specialness persists on $X'$. Maximal ordinary $\Gamma$-dimension persists as well. A positive-dimensional compact subvariety through a very general point with finite group image upstairs would have an image of the same dimension downstairs. The induced map between the normalizations is finite étale, so their fundamental-group images differ by finite index. The image downstairs would therefore have finite group image, contrary to the $\Gamma$-reduction criterion.

The Albanese map of $X'$ is surjective with connected fibres by Theorem <a href="#thm:specialness-inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:specialness-inputs">6</a>. Since $\pi_1(X')$ is torsion-free abelian, its map to the Albanese lattice $H_1(X',\mathbb Z)/\mathrm{torsion}$ is an isomorphism. The group of a smooth general Albanese fibre therefore has trivial image in $\pi_1(X')$. Maximal ordinary $\Gamma$-dimension forces that fibre to have dimension zero. Connectedness makes the general fibre one point, so the Albanese map is bimeromorphic to its torus target. This proves <span class="upright">(i)</span>. Campana’s second implication, Conjecture S to the stated bimeromorphic Iitaka conclusion, is (Claudon and Höring 2013, Remark A.4(2)) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)]; it gives <span class="upright">(ii)</span>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Holomorphic convexity of the universal cover

The compact Kähler linear Shafarevich theorem gives a further geometric consequence. Recall that a complex manifold is *holomorphically convex* if the holomorphic hull of every nonempty compact subset is compact.

<a id="stmt-cor-special-shafarevich"></a>
<div id="cor:special-shafarevich" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4** (Holomorphic convexity for special manifolds). *Let $X$ be a connected smooth compact Kähler manifold. If $X$ is special, then its ordinary universal cover is holomorphically convex.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-special-shafarevich"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-special-shafarevich</summary>

*Proof.* The group $G=\pi_1(X)$ is finitely generated because $X$ is a compact smooth manifold. By Theorem <a href="#thm:abelianity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abelianity">1</a>, it has an abelian subgroup $H$ of finite index, which is also finitely generated. Write $H\cong\mathbb Z^r\oplus T$ with $T$ finite. Decomposing $T$ into cyclic factors gives a faithful finite-dimensional complex representation $\sigma:H\to\operatorname{GL}(V)$: represent each infinite cyclic factor by powers of $2$ on its own coordinate and each finite cyclic factor by a primitive root of the corresponding order on its own coordinate. For the trivial group, use the one-dimensional trivial representation.

The induced representation $\operatorname{Ind}_H^G\sigma$ is finite dimensional because $[G:H]<\infty$, and it is faithful. Indeed, in its direct-sum realization indexed by the left cosets $G/H$, an element of its kernel must preserve the summand indexed by $H$, so it belongs to $H$; its action on that summand is given by the faithful representation $\sigma$. Thus $G$ admits a faithful finite-dimensional complex linear representation. The compact Kähler linear Shafarevich theorem of Campana–Claudon–Eyssidieux (Campana, Claudon, and Eyssidieux 2015, author’s version, Corollary 5.9) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[CCE2015](#ref-CCE2015)] now gives the claimed holomorphic convexity of the ordinary universal cover. ◻

</details>

No projectivity or maximal ordinary $\Gamma$-dimension is required here. The conclusion is holomorphic convexity, not Steinness: for example, $\mathbb P^1$ is special and its universal cover is compact. In contrast, the smooth projective surface constructed in (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[OpenAIShafarevich2026](#ref-OpenAIShafarevich2026)] has a universal cover that is not holomorphically convex. That surface is therefore not special.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Compactifiable covers

Claudon–Höring’s compactifiable-cover results give another consequence. Here a *Zariski-open* embedding is holomorphic and has closed analytic complement. The two parts require different compactifications.

<a id="stmt-cor-compactifiable-covers"></a>
<div id="cor:compactifiable-covers" class="corollary">

**Corollary 5** (Analytically compactifiable covers).

1.  *Let $X$ be a connected normal compact Kähler space and $p:V\to X$ a connected infinite étale Galois covering of complex spaces, with deck group $\Gamma$. If $V$ embeds as a Zariski-open subset of a normal compact complex space, then $\Gamma$ is virtually abelian.*

2.  *Let $X$ be a connected compact Kähler manifold and $U$ its ordinary universal cover. If $U$ embeds as a Zariski-open subset of a normal compact Kähler space, then there is a connected finite étale cover $X'\to X$ whose Albanese map $$\alpha_{X'}:X'\longrightarrow A=\mathop{\mathrm{Alb}}(X')$$ is a locally trivial holomorphic fibre bundle with simply connected compact Kähler fibre $F$. Writing $q=\dim_{\mathbb C}A$, there is a biholomorphism $$U\simeq F\times\mathbb C^q.$$ A point is allowed as $A$ or $F$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-compactifiable-covers"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-compactifiable-covers</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose (i) has a counterexample and choose one of minimal base dimension. The cover is normal because it is étale over normal $X$; normalizing an analytic compactification leaves this open set unchanged. Claudon–Höring’s minimal-counterexample reduction (Claudon and Höring 2013, Proposition 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)] says that this minimal base is smooth and special. Theorem <a href="#thm:abelianity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abelianity">1</a> makes its fundamental group virtually abelian. Its quotient $\Gamma$ is then virtually abelian, a contradiction.

For (ii), if $\pi_1(X)$ is infinite, apply (i) to the ordinary universal cover; if it is finite, it is already virtually abelian. The Kähler compactification hypothesis is the one in (Claudon and Höring 2013, Definition 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)], so (Claudon and Höring 2013, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)] gives the asserted Albanese bundle on a connected finite étale cover $X'$. Pull this bundle back along the universal cover $\mathbb C^q\to A$. The automorphism group of the compact Kähler fibre $F$ is a complex Lie group, as recalled in (Claudon and Höring 2013, sec. 2.C) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)]. The associated principal $\operatorname{Aut}(F)$-bundle is topologically trivial over the contractible base $\mathbb C^q$, and the Oka–Grauert principle makes it holomorphically trivial over this Stein base. Thus the pullback is $F\times\mathbb C^q$, the product consequence also recorded in (Claudon and Höring 2013, Conjecture 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[ClaudonHoring2013](#ref-ClaudonHoring2013)]. Since $F$ is simply connected, the pullback is the ordinary universal cover of $X'$, and hence also of $X$. ◻

</details>

In (i), the compactifying space need not be Kähler and the deck action need not extend to it. For a nonuniversal cover, only the deck group $\Gamma$, not the entire fundamental group of $X$, is controlled. The stronger Kähler compactification is used in (ii): the finite cover $X'$ need not be a product, and its fibre $F$ need not be projective.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Prior work and the remaining group-theoretic obstacle

Campana introduced specialness together with its orbifold framework and the special/general-type decomposition in (Campana 2004) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Campana2004](#ref-Campana2004)]. The same work established restrictions on solvable and linear quotients of special fundamental groups. An important advance for the full group was the proof of the abelianity conjecture for compact Kähler threefolds by Campana–Claudon (Campana and Claudon 2014, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CampanaClaudonThreefolds](#ref-CampanaClaudonThreefolds)], using the geometry of orbifold surfaces. Their later work proves specialness of general Albanese fibres in the smooth projective setting and reduces the projective conjecture to a finiteness question when all finite covers have irregularity zero (Campana and Claudon 2016, author’s version, Theorem 2.4 and Proposition 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CampanaClaudon](#ref-CampanaClaudon)]. These results explain the role of finite covers and Albanese fibre groups in the argument below. We do not assume specialness of the general Albanese fibre in the arbitrary compact Kähler case.

A classical predecessor is the case $c_1(X)=0$ in real cohomology: Beauville’s decomposition gives a finite étale cover that is a product of a complex torus and simply connected factors, and therefore gives virtual abelianity in that case (Beauville 1983, sec. 5, Theorem 2) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Beauville1983](#ref-Beauville1983)].

The linear case has a separate geometric structure theory. Building on work of Zuo and Eyssidieux, Campana–Claudon–Eyssidieux describe linear Shafarevich reductions by torus fibrations over bases of general type after finite cover and modification (Campana, Claudon, and Eyssidieux 2015, author’s version, Theorem 6.5) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[CCE2015](#ref-CCE2015)]. Their semisimple theorem gives a general-type base for the torsion-free representations used here (Campana, Claudon, and Eyssidieux 2015, author’s version, Theorems 1 and 6.3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[CCE2015](#ref-CCE2015)]. This supplies one branch of our proof. An infinite group, however, need not have an infinite finite-dimensional complex linear image. Controlling these remaining quotients is essential for a statement about the full fundamental group.

The relation between fundamental groups and holomorphic tensors has several relevant predecessors. Brunebarbe–Klingler–Totaro prove that an infinite finite-dimensional linear image over any field forces a nonzero symmetric differential on a compact Kähler manifold (Brunebarbe, Klingler, and Totaro 2013, Theorem 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[BrunebarbeKlinglerTotaro2013](#ref-BrunebarbeKlinglerTotaro2013)]. Brunebarbe–Campana prove that vanishing of all positive symmetric powers of all exterior cotangent powers forces simple connectedness; their argument develops the use of $L^2$ forms and the Poincaré–Atiyah–Gromov method (Brunebarbe and Campana 2016, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[BrunebarbeCampana2016](#ref-BrunebarbeCampana2016)]. Specialness permits many nonzero tensors, so their vanishing hypothesis differs from ours. The task here is to obtain sufficient *positivity* from tensors with unitary Hilbert space coefficients. Only after constructing a big ordinary line bundle, whose Iitaka dimension equals the dimension of the base, and establishing projectivity do we invoke the orbifold cotangent positivity theorem of Campana–Păun (Campana and Păun 2019, Theorem 7.11) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[CampanaPaun2019](#ref-CampanaPaun2019)].

The spectral step also belongs to the study of $L^2$ invariants of infinite coverings. Atiyah’s index theorem and regular-coefficient bundle interpretation (Atiyah 1976, Theorem 3.8 and Section 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Atiyah1976](#ref-Atiyah1976)], and Dodziuk’s reduced $L^2$ de Rham–Hodge theory (Dodziuk 1977) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Dodziuk1977](#ref-Dodziuk1977)], provide foundational models for working on a cover through Hilbert coefficients downstairs. Lott surveys the zero-in-the-spectrum question and several geometric cases (Lott 1996) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Lott1996](#ref-Lott1996)]. The unrestricted Riemannian assertion has counterexamples (Farber and Weinberger 2001) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[FarberWeinberger2001](#ref-FarberWeinberger2001)]; the Kähler spectral statement needed here is proved in Section <a href="#sec:spectral" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:spectral">3</a>.

A second issue is geometric descent over a possibly nonprojective torus. Campana’s correction to the Albanese theorem preserves surjectivity and connectedness for special compact Kähler manifolds, but its repaired assertion excluding multiple fibres requires a projective target (Campana 2023, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[CampanaCorrection](#ref-CampanaCorrection)]. Our proof therefore keeps the actual orders of meridians in the chosen group quotient, including on exceptional divisors of new models. Juanyong Wang’s subadditivity and Albanese theorems provide the required geometric input over arbitrary complex tori (J. Wang 2021, Theorems A(II) and C) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[JWang2021](#ref-JWang2021)]. They apply to the smooth klt pairs arising here: the boundary has simple normal crossings and rational coefficients strictly less than one.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Overview of the proof

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### The group to be excluded.

Finite étale covers preserve specialness. Since the Albanese map of each cover is surjective, its first Betti number is bounded by twice the dimension of $X$. Pass to a finite cover on which that number is maximal. Write $\alpha:X\to A=\mathop{\mathrm{Alb}}(X)$, and let $N$ be the image in $G=\pi_1(X)$ of the fundamental group of a smooth general Albanese fibre. We prove $$1\longrightarrow N\longrightarrow G\longrightarrow
 \pi_1(A)\longrightarrow1,$$ and show that $N$ is finitely generated and *FAb*: every finite-index subgroup of $N$ has finite abelianization. The latter step uses Botong Wang’s torsion theorem for isolated characters in cohomology jump loci (B. Wang 2016, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[BWang2016](#ref-BWang2016)]. It remains to exclude infinite $N$; a finite kernel of a lattice quotient gives virtual abelianity by elementary group theory.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
###### A minimal base carrying an infinite quotient.

Assuming $N$ infinite, consider fibrations of Albanese fibres on all finite covers for which the quotient by the fibre-group image is still infinite. Minimize the dimension $\ell>0$ of their bases. Compact analytic cycle spaces and Campana’s meromorphic $\Gamma$-reduction (Barlet 1975; Campana 1994) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Barlet1975](#ref-Barlet1975), [CampanaGamma](#ref-CampanaGamma)] globalize a minimizer to a factorization $$X\dashrightarrow Z\longrightarrow A.$$ If $B_0$ is the actual group image of a general fibre of the first map, then $Q=N/B_0$ is an infinite subgroup of $R=G/B_0$, and $$1\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow R\longrightarrow\pi_1(A)
 \longrightarrow1.$$ A very general fibre $Y$ of $Z\to A$ has dimension $\ell$. The actual meridian orders in $R$ restrict to the same orders in $Q$ on $Y$. For each boundary divisor $D\subset Y$, denote this finite order by $m_D$, and put $\Delta_Y=\sum_D(1-m_D^{-1})D$. The resulting orbifold $(Y,\Delta_Y)$ maps onto $Q$ on fundamental groups. Minimality holds simultaneously after the compatible finite covers and for every further infinite quotient. This is the input to the two branches in Figure <a href="#fig:proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof">1</a>.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
###### Positivity in the absence of an infinite linear image.

Theorem <a href="#thm:zero-spectrum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:zero-spectrum">13</a> first shows that an infinite normal cover of a compact Kähler orbifold has zero in the spectrum of its scalar Dolbeault Laplacian, with ordinary complex-valued coefficients, in *some* holomorphic degree. The argument places the compact diagonal on $(U\times U)/P$, where $U$ is the cover and its deck group $P$ acts diagonally. The diagonal $\{(u,u):u\in U\}$ therefore has compact quotient. Under a contrary spectral gap, weighted solutions and finite-propagation smoothing preserve the nonzero cohomology class of the diagonal after push-down, while making its pairing tend to zero. The contradiction produces the required low spectrum.

When every finite-dimensional complex linear image of $Q$ is finite, we construct nonzero holomorphic tensors with unitary Hilbert coefficients. We use the spectral construction in the nonamenable case and Shalom’s reduced-cohomology theorem in the amenable case (Shalom 2006, Theorem 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Shalom2006](#ref-Shalom2006)]. Taking exterior powers gives tensors with rank-one coefficient image; these lines define maps to Hilbert projective space. We minimize the rank among such maps, prove discreteness of the monodromy on their regular image germs, and compactify the fibres using transverse volume bounds. A rank smaller than $\ell$ would contradict the minimal-base condition. Full rank yields a big cotangent line, and hence $K_Y+\Delta_Y$ is big by Theorem <a href="#thm:hilbert-positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:hilbert-positivity">19</a>. When $Q$ instead has an infinite linear image, its FAb property produces the required semisimple image and minimality gives generic largeness. The general-type criterion of Campana–Claudon–Eyssidieux then gives $K_Y$ big.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
###### Descent and the contradiction.

Both branches give fibre positivity on every sufficiently prepared model. Theorem <a href="#thm:torus-descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:torus-descent">33</a> turns it into a positive-dimensional orbifold base of general type whose orbifold pluriforms pull back to a Bogomolov sheaf on a finite cover of $X$. The key point is to retain actual quotient meridian orders on each model; no birational invariance of the associated log Kodaira dimension is assumed. Differential vanishing cancels the boundary poles on a neat model. Specialness excludes the resulting sheaf, so $N$ is finite and Theorem <a href="#thm:abelianity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:abelianity">1</a> follows.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
###### Organization.

Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> fixes specialness, group reductions, and the precise external geometric inputs. Section <a href="#sec:spectral" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:spectral">3</a> proves the scalar spectral theorem. Section <a href="#sec:hilbert" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hilbert">4</a> constructs the Hilbert tensors and proves their positivity theorem under the minimality condition. Section <a href="#sec:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:descent">5</a> develops meridian bookkeeping and torus descent. Section <a href="#sec:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completion">6</a> establishes the Albanese exact sequence and FAb property, constructs the minimal relative base, and assembles the two representation cases to finish the proof.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Geometric and group-theoretic preliminaries

We collect the geometric input that detects a forbidden Bogomolov sheaf and the group lemmas that will convert a finite Albanese fibre image into virtual abelianity.

All manifolds are connected unless stated otherwise. A fibration is a dominant meromorphic map with connected general fibre. We resolve its graph when making differential or fundamental-group arguments. A smooth compact space in Fujiki’s class $\mathcal C$ is replaced, when needed, by a compact Kähler modification. Smooth compact bimeromorphic models have the same fundamental group. Fibre groups always mean groups of smooth models of general fibres; their images are understood up to base-point transport. The image of a general fibre group is normal in the total fundamental group: over a smooth fibration locus this follows from the homotopy exact sequence, and the fundamental group of the open total space surjects onto that of the total space.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### Specialness and quotient reductions

We recall the precise established inputs used in the proof.

<a id="stmt-thm-specialness-inputs"></a>
<div id="thm:specialness-inputs" class="theorem">

**Theorem 6** (Bogomolov–Campana). *For smooth compact Kähler manifolds, specialness in the definition of Section <a href="#sec:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:introduction">1</a> is bimeromorphically invariant and is preserved by connected finite étale covers. The Albanese morphism of a special manifold is surjective with connected fibres. Moreover, if a line bundle $L$ has a nonzero morphism to $\Omega_X^p$, then $\kappa(X,L)\le p$.*

</div>

The Bogomolov-sheaf characterization and stability under finite étale covers are given in (Campana 2004, author’s version, Theorems 2.26 and 5.12) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Campana2004](#ref-Campana2004)]; the remaining specialness properties are developed there. The Iitaka bound originates in Bogomolov’s projective work (Bogomolov 1979) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[Bogomolov1979](#ref-Bogomolov1979)]; for its compact Kähler formulation, see (Campana 2015) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[Campana2015Survey](#ref-Campana2015Survey)]. The Albanese statement is recorded with its correction in (Campana 2023, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[CampanaCorrection](#ref-CampanaCorrection)]. Related projective stability results are developed in (Campana and Claudon 2016) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[CampanaClaudon](#ref-CampanaClaudon)]. We only use surjectivity and connectedness from that correction. The meridian statement needed for arbitrary, possibly nonprojective Albanese tori will be proved in Lemma <a href="#lem:albanese-exactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:albanese-exactness">34</a>. Saturating a rank-one subsheaf and taking its double dual does not decrease its Iitaka dimension. Thus the line-bundle definition agrees with the saturated Bogomolov-sheaf formulation.

<a id="stmt-lem-torus-divisor"></a>
<div id="lem:torus-divisor" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Effective divisors on a torus). *Let $D\ne0$ be an effective rational divisor on a compact complex torus $A$. There are a quotient homomorphism $p:A\to B$ with connected fibres, where $B$ is a positive-dimensional abelian variety, and an ample effective rational divisor $D_B$ on $B$ such that $D=p^*D_B$. In particular, $\kappa(A,D)>0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-torus-divisor"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-torus-divisor</summary>

*Proof.* We give the standard torus argument; see also (Debarre 1999) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[Debarre1999](#ref-Debarre1999)]. After multiplying $D$ by a positive integer, assume it is integral. Average its positive integration current $[D]$ over translations of $A$. The result is a smooth translation-invariant semipositive $(1,1)$-form $\theta$ representing $c_1(\mathcal O_A(D))$. Write $A=V/\Lambda$. The alternating form of $\theta$ is integral on $\Lambda$, so its radical $W$ is defined over $\mathbb Q$. It is a complex subspace because $\theta$ has type $(1,1)$. Thus $K=W/(W\cap\Lambda)$ is a subtorus. Let $p:A\to B=A/K$ be the quotient.

On every $K$-coset, $\mathcal O_A(D)$ has zero real first Chern class. If its defining section is not identically zero on that coset, its zero divisor must be empty: a nonzero effective divisor on the positive-dimensional Kähler torus $K$ has strictly positive pairing with the $(\dim K-1)$-st power of a Kähler class. For $K=0$ the same conclusion is immediate. Each coset is therefore either contained in $\operatorname{Supp}D$ or disjoint from it. If $B$ were a point, this argument with $K=A$ would make the defining section of $D$ nowhere zero, contrary to $D\ne0$. Hence $\dim B>0$.

Translations by the connected group $K$ consequently preserve each prime component $D_i$ of $D$, since a connected group cannot permute their finite set. The image $\overline D_i=p(D_i)$ is a prime divisor on $B$: properness gives an analytic image, and invariance gives $\dim\overline D_i=\dim D_i-\dim K=\dim B-1$. Moreover $p^{-1}(\overline D_i)=D_i$, and smoothness of $p$ gives $p^*\overline D_i=D_i$ with multiplicity one. Keeping the coefficients of the $D_i$ therefore defines an effective integral divisor $D_B$ with $D=p^*D_B$.

Since $p^*D_B=D$, uniqueness of translation-invariant cohomology representatives identifies $c_1(\mathcal O_B(D_B))$ with the positive definite form induced by $\theta$ on $B$. The positivity criterion for line bundles on a complex torus makes $D_B$ ample and $B$ an abelian variety. Dividing $D_B$ by the initial integer proves the rational statement. Ratios of sections of its ample multiples remain independent after pullback, so $\kappa(A,D)\ge\dim B>0$. ◻
